\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert e^{-\frac{\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2}{8}}.
\]
These bounds imply \eqref{eq:gaussian-pairing}.  On
\(\{\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert\le B_\nu(t)\}\),
\[
\nu\sqrt t\,\left\lvert \nabla\mathsf{p}\right\rvert
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:grad-p-global}}{\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}}
\nu\sqrt t\,(1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert)\mathsf{p}
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:weight-scales}}{\lesssim}
\sqrt\nu\,\mathsf{p}.
\]
On \(\{\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert\ge B_\nu(t)\}\), one has \(q=0\) and
\[
\nu\sqrt t\,\left\lvert \nabla\mathsf{p}\right\rvert
=\frac{\beta}{4}\nu\sqrt t\,\mathsf{p}
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}\sqrt\nu\,\mathsf{p}.
\]
This proves \eqref{eq:nu-grad-p-small}.  For the composite profile,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left\lvert w_{2,a}\right\rvert+\left\lvert \nabla w_{2,a}\right\rvert
&\stackrel{\substack{\eqref{eq:remainder-core-composite}\\
\eqref{eq:profile-Gaussian-bounds}}}{\mathrel{\underset{\gamma,T}{\lesssim}}}
e^{-\gamma\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2/4},
\\
\left\lvert v_{2,a}\right\rvert
&\stackrel{\substack{\eqref{eq:remainder-core-composite}\\
\eqref{eq:high-profile-velocity}}}{\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}}
\frac{1}{(1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2)^{3/2}},
\end{aligned}
\]
where we used \(\nu t\le1\).
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- lemma-31: body_md -->
\label{lem:w2-corrector-vorticity}
For \(0<t\le T\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:corrector-vorticity-estimate}
t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_2\cdot\nabla w_{2,a},\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}t\mathcal K(t).
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-31 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
H\"older's inequality with exponents \(2,4,4\), followed by
\eqref{eq:v-L4}, \eqref{eq:p-comparable},
\eqref{eq:gaussian-pairing}, and
\eqref{eq:composite-profile-bounds}, gives
\begin{align*}
t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_2\cdot\nabla w_{2,a},\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
&\le
t\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\overline{w}_2\right\rVert_{L^2}
\left\lVert \overline{v}_2\right\rVert_{L^4}
\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\nabla w_{2,a}\right\rVert_{L^4}
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
t\mathcal K(t).
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- lemma-32: body_md -->
\label{lem:w2-corrector-transport}
For \(0<t\le T\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:corrector-transport-estimate}
t\left|
\left\langle v_{2,a}\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}t\mathcal K(t).
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-32 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
Since \(v_{2,a}=K\star_\xi w_{2,a}\) is divergence free,
integration by parts gives
\[
\left\langle v_{2,a}\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
=
-\frac12\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
(v_{2,a}\cdot\nabla\mathsf{p})\left\lvert \overline{w}_2\right\rvert^2\,d\xi.
\]
\[
\left\lvert v_{2,a}\cdot\nabla\mathsf{p}\right\rvert
\stackrel{\substack{\eqref{eq:grad-p-global}\\
\eqref{eq:composite-profile-bounds}}}{\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}}
\frac{1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert}{(1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2)^{\frac32}}\mathsf{p}
\lesssim\mathsf{p}.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\begin{aligned}
t\left|
\left\langle v_{2,a}\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
&\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
t\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathsf{p}\left\lvert \overline{w}_2\right\rvert^2\,d\xi
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:p-comparable}}{\lesssim}
t\mathcal K(t).
\end{aligned}
\]
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- lemma-33: body_md -->
\label{lem:w2-self-advection}
For every \(\eta>0\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:nonlinear-core-estimate}
\begin{aligned}
t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_2\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
-\eta\bigl(\mathcal I(t)+\mathcal K(t)\bigr)
\mathrel{\underset{\eta,T}{\lesssim}}{}&
t\mathcal K(t)^2.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-33 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
The velocity \(\overline{v}_2\) is divergence free, so
\[
\left\langle \overline{v}_2\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
=
-\frac12\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
(\overline{v}_2\cdot\nabla\mathsf{p})\left\lvert \overline{w}_2\right\rvert^2\,d\xi.
\]
By \eqref{eq:remainder-mass-constraints} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:v-decay},
\[
\left\lvert \overline{v}_2(\xi,t)\right\rvert
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\frac{1}{1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2}
\bigl(\mathcal I(t)+\mathcal K(t)\bigr)^{\frac12}.
\]
\[
\begin{aligned}
t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_2\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
\stackrel{\substack{\eqref{eq:grad-p-global}\\
\eqref{eq:p-comparable}}}{\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}}
t\bigl(\mathcal I+\mathcal K\bigr)^{\frac12}\mathcal K.
\end{aligned}
\]
Young's inequality, followed by \(t^2\le Tt\), gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_2\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
&\le\eta(\mathcal I+\mathcal K)
+C_{\eta,T}t^2\mathcal K^2
\\
&\le\eta(\mathcal I+\mathcal K)
+C_{\eta,T}Tt\mathcal K^2,
\end{aligned}
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:nonlinear-core-estimate}.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- lemma-34: body_md -->
\label{lem:w2-wave-G}
For every \(\eta>0\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:regular-leading-coupling}
\frac{1}{\sqrt t}\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_1(x,t)\cdot\nabla G,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
-\eta\mathcal K(t)
\mathrel{\underset{\eta,T}{\lesssim}}
t^{-1}\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-34 -->
<!-- proof-41: body_md -->
H\"older's inequality, \eqref{eq:wave-remainder-velocity-bound},
\eqref{eq:gaussian-pairing}, and \eqref{eq:p-comparable} give
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{\sqrt t}\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_1(x,t)\cdot\nabla G,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
&\le
\frac{1}{\sqrt t}
\left\lVert \overline{v}_1(t)\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\nabla G\right\rVert_{L^2}
\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\overline{w}_2\right\rVert_{L^2}
\\
&\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
t^{-\frac12}\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\mathcal K(t)^{\frac12}.
\end{align*}
Young's inequality proves \eqref{eq:regular-leading-coupling}.
<!-- end proof-41 -->
<!-- lemma-35: body_md -->
\label{lem:w2-wave-profile}
For every \(\eta>0\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:regular-profile-coupling}
\begin{aligned}
\nu\sqrt t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_1(x,t)\cdot\nabla w_{2,a},\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
-\eta\mathcal K(t)
&\mathrel{\underset{\eta,T}{\lesssim}}
\nu^2t\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2
\\
&\le
t^{-1}\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-35 -->
<!-- proof-42: body_md -->
H\"older's inequality, \eqref{eq:wave-remainder-velocity-bound},
\eqref{eq:gaussian-pairing}, \eqref{eq:composite-profile-bounds},
and \eqref{eq:p-comparable} give
\begin{align*}
\nu\sqrt t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_1(x,t)\cdot\nabla w_{2,a},\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
&\le
\nu\sqrt t\,\left\lVert \overline{v}_1(t)\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\nabla w_{2,a}\right\rVert_{L^2}
\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\overline{w}_2\right\rVert_{L^2}
\\
&\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\nu\sqrt t\,\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\mathcal K(t)^{\frac12}.
\end{align*}
Young's inequality gives the first estimate in
\eqref{eq:regular-profile-coupling}. Since \((\nu t)^2\le1\),
we have \(\nu^2t\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2\le t^{-1}\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2\), which gives the second.
<!-- end proof-42 -->
<!-- lemma-36: body_md -->
\label{lem:w2-wave-transport}
For \(0<t\le T\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:regular-core-transport}
\nu\sqrt t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_1(x,t)\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\sqrt\nu\,\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\mathcal K(t).
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-36 -->
<!-- proof-43: body_md -->
Since \(\nabla_x\cdot\overline{v}_1=0\),
\[
\nabla_\xi\cdot\overline{v}_1(x,t)
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:w2-physical-point}}{=}
\sqrt{\nu t}\,
(\nabla_x\cdot\overline{v}_1)(x,t)
=0.
\]
Therefore, integration by parts, together with \eqref{eq:wave-remainder-velocity-bound},
\eqref{eq:nu-grad-p-small}, and \eqref{eq:p-comparable}, gives
\begin{align*}
\nu\sqrt t\left|
\left\langle \overline{v}_1(x,t)\cdot\nabla\overline{w}_2,\overline{w}_2\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
&=
\frac{\nu\sqrt t}{2}
\left|
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
(\overline{v}_1(x,t)\cdot\nabla\mathsf{p})\left\lvert \overline{w}_2\right\rvert^2\,d\xi
\right|
\\
&\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\sqrt\nu\,\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\mathcal K(t).
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-43 -->
<!-- lemma-37: body_md -->
\label{lem:w2-residual}
For every \(\eta>0\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:high-residual-pairing}
\frac{1}{\nu t}\left|
\left\langle \Phi_{\mathrm{app}}(t),\overline{w}_2(t)\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
-\eta\mathcal K(t)
\mathrel{\underset{\eta,T}{\lesssim}}
\nu t.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-37 -->
<!-- proof-44: body_md -->
Fix \(\gamma\in(\frac12,1)\).
The Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, \eqref{eq:residual-bound},
\eqref{eq:gaussian-pairing}, and \eqref{eq:p-comparable} give
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{\nu t}\left|
\left\langle \Phi_{\mathrm{app}}(t),\overline{w}_2(t)\right\rangle_{\mathsf{p}(t)}
\right|
&\le
\frac{1}{\nu t}
\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\Phi_{\mathrm{app}}(t)\right\rVert_{L^2}
\left\lVert \mathsf{p}^{\frac12}\overline{w}_2(t)\right\rVert_{L^2}
\\
&\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
(\nu t)^{\frac12}\mathcal K(t)^{\frac12}.
\end{align*}
Young's inequality proves \eqref{eq:high-residual-pairing}.
<!-- end proof-44 -->
<!-- proof-45: body_md -->
Theorem~\ref{thm:linear-weighted-estimate}, the source identity
\eqref{eq:w2-linear-source}, and
Lemmas~\ref{lem:w2-corrector-vorticity}--\ref{lem:w2-residual} give
\begin{align*}
t\frac{d}{dt}E_{\mathsf{p}}
+\kappa_T\mathcal Q
-\eta(\mathcal I+\mathcal K)
-3\eta\mathcal K
\mathrel{\underset{\eta,T}{\lesssim}}{}&
tE_{\mathsf{p}}+t\mathcal K+t\mathcal K^2+t^{-1}\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2
+\sqrt\nu\,\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\mathcal K+\nu t.
\end{align*}
Choose \(\eta=\kappa_T/8\).  Since
\(\mathcal I+\mathcal K\le\mathcal Q\), the four terms proportional to
\(\eta\) are bounded by \(\frac{\kappa_T}{2}\mathcal Q\) and can be
absorbed into the left-hand side.  Moreover,
\[
\mathcal K
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:weighted-energy-comparable}}{\le}
\frac83E_{\mathsf{p}},
\]
and hence
\(t\mathcal K\lesssim tE_{\mathsf{p}}\) and
\(t\mathcal K^2\lesssim tE_{\mathsf{p}}^2\).
Taking \(\kappa_1=\frac{\kappa_T}{2}\) proves
\eqref{eq:w2-apriori}.
<!-- end proof-45 -->
<!-- lemma-38: body_md -->
\label{lem:far-field-core}
If \(t\in(0,T]\) and \(x\in\operatorname{supp}\omega^{E,\nu}_{\text{app}}(t)\), then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:far-field-xi}
\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert
=
\frac{\left\lvert x-Z^{\nu}(t)\right\rvert}{\sqrt{\nu t}}
\ge
\frac8{\sqrt{\nu t}}.
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:far-field-v2a}
\left\lvert v_{2,a}(\xi,t)\right\rvert
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
(\nu t)^{\frac{3}{2}},
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:far-field-vb2}
\left\lvert \overline{v}_2(\xi,t)\right\rvert
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\nu t\,\mathcal Q(t)^{\frac{1}{2}}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-38 -->
<!-- proof-46: body_md -->
For \(x\in\operatorname{supp}\omega^{E,\nu}_{\text{app}}(t)\),
\[
\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:w2-physical-point}}{=}
\frac{\left\lvert x-Z^{\nu}(t)\right\rvert}{\sqrt{\nu t}}
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:approx-separation}}{\ge}
\frac8{\sqrt{\nu t}},
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:far-field-xi}.  Moreover,
\[
\left\lvert v_{2,a}(\xi,t)\right\rvert
\stackrel{\substack{\eqref{eq:high-profile-velocity}\\
\eqref{eq:remainder-core-composite}}}{\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}}
\frac{1}{(1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2)^{3/2}}
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:far-field-xi}}{\lesssim}
(\nu t)^{3/2},
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:far-field-v2a}.
By \eqref{eq:remainder-mass-constraints} and
Lemma~\ref{lem:v-decay},
\[
\left\lvert \overline{v}_2(\xi,t)\right\rvert
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\frac{1}{1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2}
\left(
\mathcal I(t)+\mathcal K(t)
\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:far-field-xi}}{\lesssim}
\nu t\,\mathcal Q(t)^{1/2}.
\]
<!-- end proof-46 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop:w1-estimate}
For every \(0<t_0\le t\le T\), uniformly in \(t_0\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w1-estimate}
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t_0)\right\rVert_{}
+\int_{t_0}^t
s^{\frac32}\mathcal Q(s)^{\frac12}\,ds
+\sqrt\nu\,(t-t_0).
\end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-47: body_md -->
The regular remainder \(\overline{w}_1\) solves
\eqref{eq:wave-remainder-equation}. Its transport velocity
\(u^\nu\) is divergence free, and its forcing \(f_E\) is defined in
\eqref{eq:regular-remainder-forcing}.
The standard \(L^p\) estimate, applied with \(p=4\) and \(p=4/3\),
gives
\[
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}
\le
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t_0)\right\rVert_{}
+\int_{t_0}^t\left\lVert f_E(s)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}\,ds,
\]
where we use the norm \eqref{eq:wave-remainder-norm}.

By \eqref{eq:wave-remainder-velocity-bound} and the regularity
bounds in Proposition~\ref{prop:regular-corrector},
\[
\left\lVert \overline{v}_1\cdot\nabla\omega^{E,\nu}_{\text{app}}\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}.
\]
On the support of \(\nabla\omega^{E,\nu}_{\text{app}}\),
\eqref{eq:far-field-v2a} and \eqref{eq:far-field-vb2} yield
\[
\frac{\sqrt t}{\nu}\left\lvert v_{2,a}(\xi,t)\right\rvert
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}\sqrt\nu\,t^2
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}\sqrt\nu,
\qquad
\frac{\sqrt t}{\nu}\left\lvert \overline{v}_2(\xi,t)\right\rvert
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}t^{\frac32}\mathcal Q(t)^{\frac12}.
\]
Moreover, Proposition~\ref{prop:regular-corrector} gives
\[
\sqrt\nu\,
\left\lVert v_{1,a}\cdot\nabla w_{1,a}-\Delta w_{1,a}\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}\sqrt\nu.
\]
Combining these bounds in
\eqref{eq:regular-remainder-forcing}, we obtain
\[
\left\lVert f_E(t)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
\mathrel{\underset{T}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}
+t^{\frac32}\mathcal Q(t)^{\frac12}
+\sqrt\nu.
\]
Gronwall's inequality on \([t_0,t]\) now proves
\eqref{eq:w1-estimate}, with a constant independent of \(t_0\).
<!-- end proof-47 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:short-time-startup}
For each fixed \(0<\nu\le\nu_T\), there are \(C_\nu>0\) and a
time \(t_\nu\), with \(0<t_\nu\le\min\{T,1\}\), such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:short-time-startup}
\left\lVert h(t)\right\rVert_{Y}\le C_\nu t^{3/2},
\qquad
\left\lVert e(t)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}\le C_\nu t,
\qquad 0<t\le t_\nu.
\end{equation}
Consequently,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:short-time-remainder-traces}
E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)\le C_\nu t,
\qquad
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\le C_\nu t,
\qquad 0<t\le t_\nu.
\end{equation}
The constants and \(t_\nu\) may depend on \(\nu\).
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-48: body_md -->
Fix \(\nu>0\), and write
\[
v^e:=K\star_x e,
\qquad
v^h:=K\star_\xi h.
\]
The Gaussian bounds and standard localized energy estimates justify
the weighted integrations by parts below on every compact interval
of positive times.

We first estimate the wave error. Since \(w_2-G\) has zero mass,
the Gaussian bound in \eqref{eq:short-time-G-trace} and the
Biot--Savart formula give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:short-time-far-field}
\left\lvert v_2(\xi,t)-v^G(\xi)\right\rvert
\le\frac{C_\nu}{1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2}.
\end{equation}
Indeed, subtracting \(K(\xi)\) inside the convolution removes the
leading far-field term. By \eqref{eq:approx-separation}, it follows
that
\[
\left\lvert v^{B,\nu}(x,t)-V_G^{Z^{\nu},\nu}(x,t)\right\rvert
\le C_\nu\sqrt t,
\qquad x\in\operatorname{supp}\nabla\omega^{E,\nu}_{\text{app}}(t).
\]
Subtracting \eqref{eq:regular-approx-residual} from the exact wave
equation, and using the transport--diffusion estimate together with
\(\left\lVert v^e\right\rVert_{L^\infty}\lesssim\left\lVert e\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}\), gives
\[
\left\lVert e(t)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
\le C_\nu\int_0^t
\left(\left\lVert e(s)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\sqrt s+\nu^2\right)\,ds.
\]
Here the initial term vanishes by
\eqref{eq:short-time-wave-trace}. Gronwall's inequality yields
\[
\left\lVert e(t)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}+\left\lVert v^e(t)\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\le C_\nu t,
\qquad 0<t\le t_\nu\le1.
\]

We next estimate the core error. A direct calculation from
\eqref{eq:concentrated-remainder-equation}, using
\(h=\nu t\,\overline{w}_2\) and \(v^e=\nu^{3/2}\overline{v}_1\), gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:unscaled-core-error}
\begin{aligned}
0={}&(t\partial_t-\mathcal{L})h+\nu^{-1}\Lambda h
+\sqrt{\frac t\nu}\,b\cdot\nabla h
\\
&+t\left(v_{2,a}\cdot\nabla h
+v^h\cdot\nabla w_{2,a}\right)
+\nu^{-1}v^h\cdot\nabla h
\\
&+\sqrt{\frac t\nu}\,v^e(x,t)\cdot
\nabla(w_{2,\mathrm{app}}+h)
+\Phi_{\mathrm{app}},
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where \(x=Z^{\nu}(t)+\sqrt{\nu t}\,\xi\). Set
\[
\widehat E_h(t):=\frac12\left\lVert h(t)\right\rVert_{Y}^2,
\qquad
D_h(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathsf{p}_G
\left(\left\lvert \nabla h\right\rvert^2+(1+\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert^2)\left\lvert h\right\rvert^2\right)\,d\xi.
\]
Since \(h\) has zero mass, the Gaussian spectral gap and the
skew-symmetry of \(\Lambda\) give
\[
-\left\langle \mathcal{L} h,h\right\rangle_Y\ge cD_h,
\qquad
\left\langle \Lambda h,h\right\rangle_Y=0,
\]
as in \cite{Ga11}. In particular, \(D_h\) controls
\(\widehat E_h\).

The estimates needed for the remaining terms are
\[
\left\lvert b(\xi,t)\right\rvert\le C_T\sqrt{\nu t}\,\left\lvert \xi\right\rvert,
\qquad
\left\lVert v^h\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\le C\widehat E_h^{1/4}D_h^{1/4},
\]
and
\[
\left\|
\sqrt{\frac t\nu}\,v^e(x,t)\cdot\nabla w_{2,\mathrm{app}}
+\Phi_{\mathrm{app}}
\right\|_Y
\le C_\nu t^{3/2}.
\]
The last bound follows from the wave estimate above,
\eqref{eq:composite-profile-bounds}, and
\eqref{eq:residual-bound}. Testing
\eqref{eq:unscaled-core-error} against \(h\) in \(Y\), integrating
the divergence-free transport terms by parts, and applying Young's
inequality therefore yields
\[
t\widehat E_h'(t)+cD_h(t)
\le C_\nu tD_h(t)
+C_\nu\widehat E_h(t)^3+C_\nu t^3.
\]
The cubic term comes from \(\nu^{-1}v^h\cdot\nabla h\); the
last term is the square of the forcing bound above.

By \eqref{eq:short-time-wave-trace},
\(\widehat E_h(t)\to0\). Since \(D_h\) controls
\(\widehat E_h\), decreasing \(t_\nu\) allows us to absorb both
\(C_\nu tD_h\) and \(C_\nu\widehat E_h^3\). Hence
\[
\widehat E_h'(t)\le C_\nu t^2,
\qquad 0<t\le t_\nu.
\]
Integrating from \(\delta\) to \(t\) and letting
\(\delta\to0^+\), using \(\widehat E_h(\delta)\to0\), gives
\[
\widehat E_h(t)\le C_\nu t^3,
\qquad
\left\lVert h(t)\right\rVert_{Y}\le C_\nu t^{3/2}.
\]
Together with the wave estimate, this proves
\eqref{eq:short-time-startup}.

Finally, \(h=\nu t\,\overline{w}_2\),
\(e=\nu^{3/2}\overline{w}_1\), and \(\mathsf{p}\le2\mathsf{p}_G\) imply
\[
E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)
\le\frac{\left\lVert h(t)\right\rVert_{Y}^2}{(\nu t)^2}
\le C_\nu t,
\qquad
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}
=\nu^{-3/2}\left\lVert e(t)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
\le C_\nu t,
\]
after increasing the constant depending on \(\nu\). This proves
\eqref{eq:short-time-remainder-traces}.
<!-- end proof-48 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop:uniform-closure}
After decreasing \(\nu_T>0\) if necessary, the integral
\begin{equation}\label{eq:integrated-core-dissipation}
D(t):=\int_0^t\frac{\mathcal Q(s)}s\,ds
\end{equation}
is finite, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uniform-closure}
E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)+\kappa_1D(t)\le C_T\nu t,
\qquad
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\le C_T\sqrt\nu\,t,
\end{equation}
for \(0<\nu\le\nu_T\) and \(0<t\le T\).
The constant \(C_T\) is independent of \(\nu\).
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-49: body_md -->
We take \(\nu_T\le1\). Dividing \eqref{eq:w2-apriori} by \(t\)
gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:closure-core-energy}
\begin{aligned}
E_{\mathsf{p}}'(t)+\kappa_1\frac{\mathcal Q(t)}t
\le C_T\biggl(
&E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)+E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)^2
+\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2}{t^2}+\nu
\\
&+\sqrt\nu\,
\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}}t\,\mathcal K(t)
\biggr).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

We first verify integrability at the initial time. For fixed \(\nu\),
Theorem~\ref{lem:short-time-startup} gives
\[
E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)\le C_\nu t,
\qquad
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}\le C_\nu t,
\qquad 0<t\le t_\nu.
\]
Together with \(\mathcal K\le\frac83E_{\mathsf{p}}\), this shows that every
term on the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:closure-core-energy} is
integrable near zero. In particular,
\[
\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2}{t^2}\le C_\nu,
\qquad
\sqrt\nu\,\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}}t\,\mathcal K(t)
\le C_\nu t.
\]
Integrating \eqref{eq:closure-core-energy} from \(\delta\) to \(t\)
and letting \(\delta\to0^+\), nonnegativity of the energy,
\(E_{\mathsf{p}}(\delta)\to0\), and monotone convergence for the
dissipation give \(D(t)<\infty\). Consequently,
\[
E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)\to0,
\qquad
D(t)\to0,
\qquad t\to0^+.
\]

We can now pass to the initial time in the wave estimate.
Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_0^t s^{3/2}\mathcal Q(s)^{1/2}\,ds
&=\int_0^t s^2
\left(\frac{\mathcal Q(s)}s\right)^{1/2}\,ds
\\
&\le
\left(\int_0^t s^4\,ds\right)^{1/2}D(t)^{1/2}
\\
&=\frac{t^{5/2}}{\sqrt5}D(t)^{1/2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus, letting \(t_0\to0^+\) in \eqref{eq:w1-estimate} and using
\(\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t_0)\right\rVert_{}\to0\), we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:w1-from-zero}
\begin{aligned}
\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}
&\le C_T\left(
\int_0^t s^{3/2}\mathcal Q(s)^{1/2}\,ds
+\sqrt\nu\,t
\right)
\\
&\le C_T
\left(t^{5/2}D(t)^{1/2}+\sqrt\nu\,t\right),
\\
\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2}{t^2}
&\le C_T\left(\nu+t^3D(t)\right).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}

The last inequality controls the singular wave source by
\(\nu\) and the accumulated core dissipation. We therefore combine
the energy and the dissipation by setting
\begin{equation}\label{eq:combined-bootstrap-norm}
Y_*(t):=E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)+\kappa_1D(t),
\qquad
Y_*(0):=0.
\end{equation}
This function is continuous on \([0,T]\), locally absolutely
continuous on \((0,T]\), and satisfies
\[
Y_*'(t)
=E_{\mathsf{p}}'(t)+\kappa_1\frac{\mathcal Q(t)}t
\]
for almost every \(t>0\).
Young's inequality and
\eqref{eq:weighted-energy-comparable} give
\[
\sqrt\nu\,
\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}}t\,\mathcal K(t)
\le
\frac12\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2}{t^2}
+C\nu E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)^2.
\]
Substituting this bound and \eqref{eq:w1-from-zero} into
\eqref{eq:closure-core-energy}, and using
\[
\nu\le1,\qquad t\le T,\qquad
E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)\le Y_*(t),\qquad
D(t)\le\kappa_1^{-1}Y_*(t),
\]
we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:closed-bootstrap-ode}
Y_*'(t)
\le C_T\left(\nu+Y_*(t)+Y_*(t)^2\right)
\end{equation}
for almost every \(t\in(0,T]\).

On any interval starting at zero on which \(Y_*\le1\),
\eqref{eq:closed-bootstrap-ode} gives
\[
Y_*'(t)\le C_T\nu+2C_TY_*(t).
\]
Applying Gronwall's inequality from \(\delta>0\) and letting
\(\delta\to0^+\), we find
\[
Y_*(t)
\le\frac{\nu}{2}\left(e^{2C_Tt}-1\right)
\le C_T'\nu t.
\]
Choose \(\nu_T\) so that \(C_T'\nu_TT<1/2\).
Since \(Y_*(0)=0\) and \(Y_*\) is continuous, it cannot reach
\(1\) on \([0,T]\). Hence
\[
E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)+\kappa_1D(t)\le C_T\nu t
\]
throughout this interval. Returning to \eqref{eq:w1-from-zero},
we obtain
\[
\frac{\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}^2}{t^2}
\le C_T\left(\nu+t^3D(t)\right)
\le C_T\nu,
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:uniform-closure}.
The fixed-\(\nu\) estimates were used only to justify integration
from zero; the constants in \eqref{eq:uniform-closure} are
independent of \(\nu\).
<!-- end proof-49 -->
<!-- proof-50: body_md -->
Under the normalization
\eqref{eq:normalized-limiting-separation},
the decomposition \eqref{eq:remainder-ansatz},
Proposition~\ref{prop:regular-corrector}, and
\eqref{eq:uniform-closure} give
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left\lVert \omega^{E,\nu}(t)-\omega^E(t)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
&\le
\nu\left\lVert w_{1,a}(t)\right\rVert_{L^{4}\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
+\nu^{3/2}\left\lVert \overline{w}_1(t)\right\rVert_{}
\\
&\le C_T(\nu+\nu^2t)
\le C_T\nu.
\end{aligned}
\]
Both regular components equal \(\omega_0^E\) at \(t=0\), so this
estimate holds on \([0,T]\).

The self-similar change of variables preserves the \(L^1\) norm.
Moreover, the lower bound in \eqref{eq:p-global-bounds} gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left\lVert \overline{w}_2(t)\right\rVert_{L^1_\xi}
&\le
\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathsf{p}(t,\xi)^{-1}\,d\xi\right)^{1/2}
\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathsf{p}(t,\xi)|\overline{w}_2(\xi,t)|^2\,d\xi\right)^{1/2}
\\
&\le C_T E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)^{1/2}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Therefore, by \eqref{eq:concentrated-approximation-error},
\eqref{eq:remainder-ansatz}, and \eqref{eq:uniform-closure},
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left\lVert \omega^{B,\nu}(t)-\Gamma_{\nu t,Z^{\nu}(t)}\right\rVert_{L^1}
&=\left\lVert w_2(t)-G\right\rVert_{L^1_\xi}
\\
&\le C_T\nu t+\nu t\left\lVert \overline{w}_2(t)\right\rVert_{L^1_\xi}
\\
&\le C_T\nu t\left(1+E_{\mathsf{p}}(t)^{1/2}\right)
\\
&\le C_T\nu t.
\end{aligned}
\]

The center estimate \eqref{eq:main-center} follows from
\eqref{eq:regular-corrector-bounds}:
\[
|Z^{\nu}(t)-z(t)|\le C_T\nu t.
\]
For \(s>0\) and \(a,b\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\), integration of the directional
derivative of the Gaussian along the segment joining \(a\) and
\(b\) gives
\[
\left\lVert \Gamma_{s,a}-\Gamma_{s,b}\right\rVert_{L^1}
\le\frac{|a-b|}{\sqrt{\pi s}}.
\]
Taking \(s=\nu t\), \(a=Z^{\nu}(t)\), and \(b=z(t)\), we obtain
\[
\left\lVert \Gamma_{\nu t,Z^{\nu}(t)}-\Gamma_{\nu t,z(t)}\right\rVert_{L^1}
\le C_T\sqrt{\nu t}.
\]
Since \(\nu t\le1\), the triangle inequality proves
\eqref{eq:main-z-centered}. For each fixed \(t>0\), the asserted
inviscid limits follow from these estimates and the weak-* convergence
\[
\Gamma_{\nu t,z(t)}\to\delta_{z(t)}
\qquad\text{as }\nu\to0.
\]

Finally, undoing the scaling
\eqref{eq:parabolic-normalization} and absorbing the fixed powers
of \(\ell\) into the constants gives the estimates in the original
variables.
<!-- end proof-50 -->
<!-- lemma-39: body_md -->
\label{lem:corrector-estimates}
Let \(g=g(t)\) be a smooth family of functions supported in a fixed
compact set, with all spatial derivatives bounded uniformly in time.

For every
\(f\in W^{4,4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap W^{3,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BS-high}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{3,\infty}}
+\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
\lesssim
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}}
,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{align}
&\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}}
\notag\\
&\qquad\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}}
.
\label{eq:nonlocal-product-high}
\end{align}
For every
\(f\in W^{2,4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap W^{1,1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BS-low}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{2,\infty}}
+\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{3,4}}
\lesssim
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}
,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{align}
&\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}
\notag\\
&\qquad\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}
.
\label{eq:nonlocal-product-low}
\end{align}

Let \(V=V(t)\) be divergence free.  If \(\partial_tf+V\cdot\nabla f=F\),
then
\begin{align}
\frac d{dt}
\left(
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}}
\right)
&\lesssim
\left\lVert V\right\rVert_{W^{5,\infty}}
\left(
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}}
\right)
\notag\\
&\quad
+\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}},
\label{eq:transport-high}
\\
\frac d{dt}
\left(
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}
\right)
&\lesssim
\left\lVert V\right\rVert_{W^{3,\infty}}
\left(
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}
\right)
\notag\\
&\quad
+\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert F\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}.
\label{eq:transport-low}
\end{align}
These differential inequalities hold for almost every \(t\in[0,T]\),
and their integrated forms hold on every subinterval of \([0,T]\).
<!-- end lemma-39 -->
<!-- proof-51: body_md -->
\noindent\emph{Biot--Savart estimates.}
We first claim that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BS-zero-order}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{L^4}
+\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\lesssim
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^4}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^1},
\end{equation}
and, for every integer \(j\ge1\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:BS-derivatives}
\left\lVert \nabla^j(K\star f)\right\rVert_{L^4}
\mathrel{\underset{j}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert \nabla^{j-1}f\right\rVert_{L^4}.
\end{equation}

Since \(\left\lvert K(x)\right\rvert=(2\pi\left\lvert x\right\rvert)^{-1}\),
\[
K\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lvert x\right\rvert<1\}}
\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap L^{\frac{4}{3}}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),
\qquad
K\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lvert x\right\rvert\ge1\}}
\in L^4(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap L^\infty(\mathbb{R}^{2}).
\]
Young's inequality therefore gives
\begin{align*}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{L^4}
&\le
\left\lVert K\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lvert x\right\rvert<1\}}\right\rVert_{L^1}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^4}
+
\left\lVert K\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lvert x\right\rvert\ge1\}}\right\rVert_{L^4}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^1},
\\
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
&\le
\left\lVert K\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lvert x\right\rvert<1\}}\right\rVert_{L^{\frac{4}{3}}}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^4}
+
\left\lVert K\mathbf 1_{\{\left\lvert x\right\rvert\ge1\}}\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^1}.
\end{align*}
This proves \eqref{eq:BS-zero-order}.

To prove \eqref{eq:BS-derivatives}, let \(\alpha\) be a multi-index with
\(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert=j\ge1\), and choose \(k\) such that \(\alpha_k\ge1\).
In Fourier variables,
\[
\widehat{\partial^\alpha(K\star f)}(\xi)
=
m_k(\xi)
\widehat{\partial^{\alpha-e_k}f}(\xi),
\]
where \(m_k\) is a homogeneous multiplier of degree zero, smooth on
\(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\).  It is therefore a Calder\'on--Zygmund
multiplier, and
\[
\left\lVert \partial^\alpha(K\star f)\right\rVert_{L^4}
\mathrel{\underset{\alpha}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert \partial^{\alpha-e_k}f\right\rVert_{L^4}.
\]
Summing over \(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert=j\) proves
\eqref{eq:BS-derivatives}.

\[
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
\stackrel{\substack{\eqref{eq:BS-zero-order}\\
\eqref{eq:BS-derivatives}}}{\lesssim}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{3,4}}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^1}.
\]
Moreover, \(W^{1,4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^\infty(\mathbb{R}^{2})\), so, for
\(0\le j\le3\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left\lVert \nabla^j(K\star f)\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
&\lesssim
\left\lVert \nabla^j(K\star f)\right\rVert_{L^4}
+
\left\lVert \nabla^{j+1}(K\star f)\right\rVert_{L^4}
\\
&\stackrel{\substack{\eqref{eq:BS-zero-order}\\
\eqref{eq:BS-derivatives}}}{\lesssim}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{3,4}}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{L^1}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Together with the preceding \(W^{4,4}\)-estimate, this proves
\eqref{eq:BS-high}.  The same argument through order three in \(L^4\)
and order two in \(L^\infty\) proves \eqref{eq:BS-low}.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Product estimates.}
The product estimates \eqref{eq:nonlocal-product-high} and
\eqref{eq:nonlocal-product-low} follow from Leibniz' rule, the
Biot--Savart estimates, and the compact support of \(g\).

Indeed, for every multi-index \(\alpha\),
\[
\partial^\alpha\bigl((K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\bigr)
=
\sum_{\beta\le\alpha}
\binom{\alpha}{\beta}
\partial^\beta(K\star f)\cdot
\partial^{\alpha-\beta}\nabla g.
\]
All derivatives of \(g\) are uniformly bounded.  Hence, for
\(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le4\),
\[
\left\lVert 
\partial^\alpha\bigl((K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\bigr)\right\rVert_{L^4}
\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}.
\]
If \(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le3\), every term is supported in the fixed compact
support of \(g\).  H\"older's inequality on that compact set gives
\[
\left\lVert 
\partial^\alpha\bigl((K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\bigr)\right\rVert_{L^1}
\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{3,4}}.
\]
Summing these estimates gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}}
\\
&\qquad\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}
+\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{3,4}}
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:BS-high}}{\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{4,4}}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{3,1}}
,
\end{aligned}
\]
which is \eqref{eq:nonlocal-product-high}.

Taking instead \(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le2\) in \(L^4\) and
\(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le1\) in \(L^1\) gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert (K\star f)\cdot\nabla g\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}
\\
&\qquad\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}
\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}
+\left\lVert K\star f\right\rVert_{W^{1,4}}
\stackrel{\eqref{eq:BS-low}}{\mathrel{\underset{g}{\lesssim}}}
\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{2,4}}+\left\lVert f\right\rVert_{W^{1,1}}
,
\end{aligned}
\]
which proves \eqref{eq:nonlocal-product-low}.

\smallskip
\noindent\emph{Transport estimates.}
To prove \eqref{eq:transport-high} and \eqref{eq:transport-low}, we
commute spatial derivatives with the transport equation.

For any multi-index \(\alpha\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:differentiated-transport}
\partial_t\partial^\alpha f
+V\cdot\nabla\partial^\alpha f
=
\partial^\alpha F
-
\sum_{0<\beta\le\alpha}
\binom{\alpha}{\beta}
\partial^\beta V\cdot
\nabla\partial^{\alpha-\beta}f.
\end{equation}
For \(p=4\), test by
\(\left\lvert \partial^\alpha f\right\rvert^{2}\partial^\alpha f\) and integrate.
Since \(\nabla\cdot V=0\),
\[
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
V\cdot\nabla\partial^\alpha f\,
\left\lvert \partial^\alpha f\right\rvert^{2}\partial^\alpha f\,dx
=
\frac14\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
V\cdot\nabla\left\lvert \partial^\alpha f\right\rvert^{4}\,dx
=0.
\]
Consequently,
\begin{align}
\frac d{dt}\left\lVert \partial^\alpha f\right\rVert_{L^4}
&\stackrel{\eqref{eq:differentiated-transport}}{\mathrel{\underset{\alpha}{\lesssim}}}
\left\lVert \partial^\alpha F\right\rVert_{L^4}
\notag\\
&\quad+
\sum_{0<\beta\le\alpha}
\left\lVert \partial^\beta V\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\left\lVert 
\nabla\partial^{\alpha-\beta}f\right\rVert_{L^4}.
\label{eq:transport-L4-derivative}
\end{align}
Since \(\left\lvert \beta\right\rvert\ge1\), \(\left\lvert \alpha-\beta\right\rvert+1\le\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\),
so the right-hand side contains no derivative of \(f\) above the order
being estimated.

For the \(L^1\)-estimate, use
\(\Phi_\varepsilon(s)=\sqrt{s^2+\varepsilon^2}-\varepsilon\).
Then
\[
0\le\Phi_\varepsilon(s)\le\left\lvert s\right\rvert,
\qquad
\left\lvert \Phi_\varepsilon'(s)\right\rvert\le1,
\qquad
\Phi_\varepsilon(s)\to\left\lvert s\right\rvert.
\]
Testing by \(\Phi_\varepsilon'(\partial^\alpha f)\) gives
\[
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
V\cdot\nabla
\Phi_\varepsilon(\partial^\alpha f)\,dx
=0
\]
because \(V\) is divergence free.  Hence
\begin{align*}
\frac d{dt}
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Phi_\varepsilon(\partial^\alpha f)\,dx
&\stackrel{\eqref{eq:differentiated-transport}}{\mathrel{\underset{\alpha}{\lesssim}}}
\left\lVert \partial^\alpha F\right\rVert_{L^1}
\\
&\quad+
\sum_{0<\beta\le\alpha}
\left\lVert \partial^\beta V\right\rVert_{L^\infty}
\left\lVert 
\nabla\partial^{\alpha-\beta}f\right\rVert_{L^1}.
\end{align*}
Letting \(\varepsilon\to0\) gives the corresponding estimate for
\(\left\lVert \partial^\alpha f\right\rVert_{L^1}\).

Summing the \(L^4\)-estimates for \(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le4\) and the
\(L^1\)-estimates for \(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le3\) proves
\eqref{eq:transport-high}.  Summing them for
\(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le2\) and \(\left\lvert \alpha\right\rvert\le1\), respectively, proves
\eqref{eq:transport-low}.
<!-- end proof-51 -->
